# Persistence program — coordination ledger

Two agents work this folder: the **chat session** (cloud container, 2 cores; writes new files here
through the owner's device bridge) and the **Cairn session** (Claude Code on the Mac, native). Both
read this file; each appends to its own section under "Log". Nobody edits the other's section.
The owner owns commits; nothing here is staged or committed by either agent.

## Ownership (who changes what)

| area | owner | rule |
|------|-------|------|
| `tier0/` engine semantics: tier0v2.py, descent.py, lattice*.py, fastlevel.py, run_c.py, run_trees_c.py | chat | algorithmic changes; every change re-validated on the 53-vertex set (`runall_final.jsonl`) with zero verdict differences before it lands in `tier0/` |
| `tier0-opt/`: constant-factor optimization of lvl12.c with golden checks | Cairn | may replace `tier0/lvl12.c` only when `check_golden.sh` says SAME on every golden and the 53-vertex validation reproduces; note it in the Log |
| the Mac sweep (`tier0/trees_mac.jsonl`, `.log`) | Cairn starts/stops; chat reads | never run two sweeps into the same output; a restart uses the identical command (resumable) |
| the cloud sweep (`trees_c.jsonl`, below 3×10^11 patterns) | chat | serves as independent replication of the Mac's cheap vertices; results pushed here when done |
| control corpus + mutation tests | chat | results in `tier0/MUTATION.md` |
| audits, beads, Lean grounding, PARI re-enumeration | Cairn | per `cairn_prompt_3` |
| research notes (`IDEAS.md`, `RESULT.md`) | chat writes; Cairn may append a dated section | |

The Baker chain's exponential-in-k shape comes from the constant C ≫ 1 in gap ≤ C·h(rest)·log B; any abc-type hypothesis with exponent 1 + o(1) makes every gap ≤ ε·n + C_ε and the chain polynomial. The weakest EXPLICIT hypothesis that works: RST[C₁] — Robert–Stewart–Tenenbaum's refined abc (Bull. LMS 46 (2014), Conj. A, fetched from Tenenbaum's site) with its unspecified constant C₁ fixed (headline C₁ = 1 > β = 0.5968, their model value; consistent with every known abc triple for any C₁ ≥ 0 — a violation needs merit > 48 vs the record 38.67). Theorem C (all inequalities written out, every N(k) verified by evaluating the master inequality at N(k) and N(k)+1): under RST[1], a zero-free {2,3,7}-unit with k non-1 digits has at most N(k) digits: N(29) = 3433, N(31) = 3832, N(34) = 4466, N(35) = 4687, N(36) = 4912, N(42) = 6363, N(44) = 6883. - cairn 2026-10-21 20:36 EDT — NO-VANISHING-SUBSUM CHECK (Codex, read-only): TRUE for every admissible solution, no exceptions, n ≥ 1, every k ≥ 0. Proof (elementary, uses only positivity): with P_0 = 10^n, P_j = 9(d_j − 1)10^{e_j}, every P_i ≥ 9 and ΣP_i = 9y + 1; a nonempty proper subset either has no negative term (sum > 0), contains −1 only (positives would sum to 1 < 9), contains −9y only (the complementary positives would sum to 1), or contains both (positivity forces every positive term in, i.e. the full equation). e_k = 0 and repeated digits are covered (equal coefficients carry the same sign). k = 0: the only {2,3,7}-unit repunit is R_1 = 1 (R_n odd ⇒ a = 0; 7 | R_n ⇒ 6 | n ⇒ 11 | R_n; n ≥ 3 ⇒ R_n ≡ 7 mod 8 ≠ 3^b mod 8; R_2 = 11). Theorem applied: Evertse (Compositio 53, 1984, Thm 1) / van der Poorten–Schlickewei / Evertse–Schlickewei–Schmidt — over K = Q, T = {∞, 2, 3, 5, 7} (rank 4), N = k + 3 terms with fixed coefficients (1, 9(d_j − 1), −1, −9) in the variables (10^n, 10^{e_j}, 1, y); the −1 term's coordinate is exactly 1, so projectivization loses nothing; finiteness is per digit multiset (k fixed) and ineffective. Explicit COUNT bound (Amoroso–Viada, Duke Math. J., Thm 6.2, A(m, r) = (8m)^{4m^4(m+r+1)} for c_1x_1 + … + c_mx_m = 1, x ∈ Γ of rank r; here m = k + 2, Γ = ⟨10⟩^{k+1} × ⟨2,3,7⟩, r = k + 4): at most (8(k+2))^{4(k+2)^4(2k+7)} children per multiset — a count, not a length bound. Codex reports checking both theorem statements in the sources (Evertse via numdam, ESS arXiv math/0409604, Amoroso–Viada via iris.unito.it); Cairn has not re-read them.
